- **NEW (2026-07) — Nvidia's own NDR deck makes the "tokens = the monetizable unit / tokens-per-watt = AI-factory ROI" case with hard cost-per-token marks (NVIDIA "NDR deck", 2026-07).** NVDA's July non-deal-roadshow deck (company IR, no rating/PT) frames the whole business around the theme: **"AI factories convert data and energy into intelligence — monetizable tokens"** and **"performance per watt determines the inference economics of a power-limited data center."** The supply-side cost-per-token declines, quantified on SemiAnalysis InferenceX (DeepSeek R1 0528, FP4, 1K/1K): GB300 NVL72 delivers **35x lower cost per token vs Hopper** (agentic/low-latency), **1.5x lower cost per token vs GB200** on long-context, and **50x higher throughput per megawatt** vs the H200 NVL8 competition — i.e. tokens/watt is the deck's explicit figure-of-merit for factory ROI, the same tokens-per-MW frame the SemiAnalysis DeepSeek-V4 and CoreWeave/Cantor bullets below track. On agentic demand: GB300 NVL72 runs **up to 20x more agents per MW than H200** (DeepSeek V4 Pro, real coding-agent trajectories), already in production at **Baseten, DeepInfra, Together AI (Cursor on Blackwell)**. The demand side: **token generation growing exponentially** — OpenRouter tokens/month rising through ~123T+ (from ~3T in Jan-2025 on the deck's chart) — and **"useful AI means tokens are now profitable, which incentivizes data-center operators to expand compute"** (GitHub Apr-2026: 1.4B commits ~3x, 90M PRs merged, 20M new repos/month; ~30-40M devs / ~$3T salaries producing ~3x output ≈ $9T productivity). Read-through: this is company (not sell-side) framing but it is the clearest first-party statement of the tokenmaxxing/Jevons thesis — cheaper cost/token + more tokens/watt → more monetizable tokens → more compute demand — corroborating the on-disk MS Byrd (−75% token cost Blackwell→Feynman) and SemiAnalysis (GB300 $0.156/Mtok) bullets. No prior number superseded; additive first-party evidence. [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NDR_July2026_.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NDR_July2026_.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-06) — Nvidia's GPU backstop is explicitly a "broaden compute access" program — merchant AI-compute supply expanding beyond the hyperscalers (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity", 2026-07-06).** The stated #1 objective of Nvidia's new backstop program is **"to broaden compute availability"** along two dimensions — **(i) open the compute market well beyond a few large hyperscalers and AI labs, and (ii) make rental contracts of varying tenor available, not just 5-year terms — particularly short terms of <1 year** (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-06). This matters to the tokenmaxxing/monetization thesis because it is a structural push on the **supply and economics of merchant AI compute**: by revenue-guaranteeing Neoclouds (leveraging NVDA's AA/Aa2 balance sheet), NVDA makes non-hyperscaler clusters financeable, "broadens the base of buyers beyond just a few hyperscalers," and unlocks short-tenor rentals that inference providers (who "are completely unwilling to sign for longer than 1y") actually want — i.e. more, cheaper, more-flexible compute available to the token economy. The debate this reinforces: **GPU residual value and the dominance of the 5-year offtake template limit short-term rental liquidity** — "it is still a seller's market" for shorter-dated rentals (only a few Neoclouds still offer 1y rentals, some requiring prepays up to 100% of contract value), and there is "no active derivatives market to provide a pricing signal or a good reference for GPU residual value." So the backstop expands merchant supply/access but the short-tenor liquidity that would most directly loosen token-serving economics is still constrained (SemiAnalysis "Nvidia GPU Debt Backstop Unleashes the AI Project Trinity: Capital, Offtake and Datacenters", 2026-07-06; see also [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md), [../NVDA.md](../NVDA.md)).
- **NEW (2026-05-27, backfilled) — SemiAnalysis "Tokenomics 2.0": AWS pulled ahead on margins via the Bedrock/Anthropic *token-as-a-service* mix; Anthropic ARR $9B→$30B in 1Q26 (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27).** The clearest quantification yet that frontier-model *distribution* is where the monetization accrues: AWS **EBIT margins +213bp Q/Q** in 1Q26, driven primarily by customer spend on **Claude through Bedrock** — AWS's higher share of 3P model-API spend + the Anthropic/Bedrock deal structure (flat IaaS fee + revenue share + performance hurdles) + Anthropic's ARR outperformance. Under model *distribution* economics, **Anthropic books full token revenue as seller-of-record while AWS earns an infra fee + a distribution/rev-share fee** — the piece that boosts AWS margins above a plain IaaS rental. TaaS businesses (Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex) at the top three are **$10B+ ARR each today vs "practically nothing" at Oracle/neoclouds**, and TaaS margins are "significantly higher" than wholesale IaaS — so the frontier-distribution advantage is *widening* the gap among hyperscalers. **Anthropic added $21B net-new ARR in 1Q26 to reach $30B** (Claude Code + enterprise API the driver, API revenue ~13x y/y), inference gross margins **mid-60s% (up from 38% in 2025, -94% in 2024)**, OI-profitable-ex-SBC expected 2Q, potential for **>$100B ARR by year-end** (SemiAnalysis Tokenomics Model). Bedrock is a ~**$5.5B run-rate** business (80-90%+ Anthropic models), ~37% of AWS AI revenue. This is the strongest on-disk rebuttal to the "tokens diffuse to cheap/open-source" bear: the *revenue* keeps concentrating in the frontier + its highest-margin distribution channel (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth and Bedrock Mix Drive AWS Margins Higher", 2026-05-27). _(Backfilled on the 2026-07-02 ingest run; corroborates the BG2Pod "frontier = 90% of economic value" bullet below. See also [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md), [../AMZN.md](../AMZN.md), [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md).)_
- **NEW (2026-07-02) — Meta's four compute monetization use-cases, incl. a "Bedrock 2.0" TaaS via Anthropic + MSL frontier training (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute", 2026-07-02).** SemiAnalysis lays out why Meta can keep aggressively contracting compute ("a CFO's dream"): four high-value, differentiated use-cases — **(1) MSL frontier-model training** (the bulk of incremental capacity still goes to Meta Superintelligence Labs); **(2) RecSys** (Meta thinks it can scale ads-recommendation compute **>10x**; GEM ads foundation model beat prior baselines ~66%, ad impressions +19% / price-per-ad +12% y/y in 1Q26); **(3) a "Bedrock 2.0" token-as-a-service** — Meta reportedly in **final talks with Anthropic for private Claude instances** (like AWS Bedrock / Azure Foundry / Google Vertex), launching a token endpoint and moving up-stack (own models externally + Anthropic internally initially); **(4) SpaceX-style on-demand compute** at a huge pricing premium — SpaceX's deals price at **triple/quadruple peers' revenue-per-MW** (~$50B/GW of annual revenue; **200MW ≈ >$10B/yr**), with a 90-day bilateral cancellation clause that lets a lab claw compute back to research. The token-optimization read-through: Meta becoming a frontier-model *distributor* (Claude tokens through Meta's ad-network distribution) is another channel where frontier-model monetization compounds — while MSL keeps the option to repurpose that compute back to frontier training (SemiAnalysis "Meta Compute: Everyone Wants To Be A Cloud", 2026-07-02). _(See [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md) / [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md) for the capex/GW legs.)_
- **NEW (2026-07-08) — the definitive monetization proof: Anthropic 3Q26 GAAP profit >$1B on >$60B ARR, and the API-consumption model IS the edge (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic 3Q26 Profit Over $1B", 2026-07-08).** SemiAnalysis's bottoms-up Tokenomics model (per the note, "confirmed" by a recent WSJ piece) turns the tokenmaxxing thesis from a demand story into a printed-P&L story: **Anthropic + OpenAI combine for ~$100B of ARR**, and Anthropic — "the clear winner in the profitable monetization of AI models in 2026" as Claude Code took over software development (**>7% of total GitHub commits**) — posts **3Q26 GAAP EBIT ~$1B (6% margin)** while OpenAI sits at ~−100% EBIT. The mechanism is the theme itself: **usage-based API is 75-85% of Anthropic revenue (subscriptions just 15% of ARR; Consumer only ~5% vs OpenAI ~40%)**, and *because API is billed by token not by seat there is no ceiling on revenue per user* — as a customer adopts more agentic workflows it consumes more tokens and generates more revenue without adding a customer (the on-page "seat→usage" repricing, taken to its endpoint). **NNARR is now $10B+/month**; **NRR ~500%** (Krishna Rao: of $30B 1Q26 ARR, ~$12B from ≥1yr customers that were ~$2B a year earlier → ~$18B new-customer ARR still ramping). The Jevons/tokenmaxxing supply-side shows up in the margin: **blended token pricing has fallen** even as new models price higher (cache rates, input/output ratios, agentic-vs-chat mix), yet **blended inference GM is mid-60s% (from −94% in 2024)** and **Opus 4.8 per-token API GM is 80%+** because compute cost per unit is ~fixed while tokens/unit and price/token rise — "incremental margins approach 100%." **ARR-per-MW reaching ~$60M later this year vs ~$16M just 9 months ago.** OpenAI's disadvantage is the tokenmaxxing model in reverse: ~900M free users at ~$0.70/mo are a 20-30% blended-GM drag (at $100B ARR, ~$25B less gross profit than Anthropic to reinvest) — though its **API/B2B is now the majority of monthly NNARR** since the March 5.5/Codex releases, i.e. converging toward Anthropic's consumption model. SemiAnalysis's steady-state: 30-40% EBIT/FCF margins, GM into the mid-70s. The bull governor: pricing power/power-law usage stay in the frontier's favor *only if* the gap between SOTA and open-source/other labs stays wide (Meta/Google turning coding into a 4-horse race would pressure token pricing/GM). The stated risk: "Fable-style government delays" hurting frontier ROIC (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic 3Q26 Profit Over $1B: The Anthropic IPO Financials Sneak Peak", Brookhart/Huang/Patel, 2026-07-08). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Fw-_Anthropic_3Q26_Profit_Over_1B-_The_Anthropic_IPO_Financials_Sneak_Peak.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Fw-_Anthropic_3Q26_Profit_Over_1B-_The_Anthropic_IPO_Financials_Sneak_Peak.html) · [../ANTHROPIC.md](../ANTHROPIC.md) · [../OPENAI.md](../OPENAI.md)
- **The frontier is still winning the revenue, even as tokens diffuse.** BG2Pod (Gerstner/Baker, 2026-06-12): "Frontier might be 90% of the economic value; open source might be 80% of tokens" — the early-2025 bear case that cheap open-source tokens would close the gap on frontier has been "decisively wrong, probably more than 90%." Long-running agentic tasks (Fable 5 / Mythos / GPT-5.5) are the unlock driving more token consumption "as far as the eye can see."
- **"A big speed bump" — the token-cost backlash is now landing with frequency (UBS, 2026-06-18).** Keirstead's team aggregated 10–15 enterprise-IT checks into their first "token cost pushback" note; initially framed as a "small optimization speed bump, no change to the secular trend," but Databricks' Ali corrected him: "you're mostly right… it IS a speed bump, but it's NOT a small speed bump — it's a big speed bump," and enterprises (not just AI-natives) are now "considering cheap open-source Chinese models deployed in a contained way" as an alternative to closed frontier models. **Palantir launched "AIP Evolve" (an AI cost-optimization tool) ~3 weeks ago to 90% adoption**; Databricks sees the same spike in interest in cutting AI costs. Mitigants: many enterprises won't throttle employees this early (cut elsewhere to fund token-maxxing — "that's how UBS is behaving"); Blackwell-trained models will be more token-efficient (Arcuri). The open question Keirstead poses: can the **software industry insert itself as the "AI optimizer"** — a role the frontier labs are dis-incentivized to play (UBS "Karl software call", 2026-06-18). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html)
- **UBS "Part 2" — the impact map by tech layer (UBS, 2026-06-23).** Keirstead/Arcuri's follow-up ("The Ramp in Token Optimization") puts **~60% of enterprises now throttling AI spend** via guardrails and ranks the impact by layer: **(1) model layer takes the biggest hit** — cost-conscious enterprises down-tier to cheaper/SLM/open-source Chinese models (share gains for **Alibaba Qwen, Z.ai, Moonshot/Kimi, DeepSeek**), a shift now reaching F500 (e.g. BMW+Qwen), especially non-coding; the model market is bifurcating; **(2) semis/hardware — no material risk** (capex is frontier-lab-driven; Blackwell-trained/inferenced models bring "much better token economics"; multimodal adds upside), the lone caveat that "**AI price sensitivity could limit near-term GPU price increases**"; **(3) hyperscalers neutral** — already multi-model platforms facing a wall of demand, but token optimization "could impact frontier model API revs growth"; being multi-model = an edge "similar to the advantage secured by the likes of Snowflake and MongoDB that were multi-cloud while AWS and Microsoft were pushing their native databases"; **(4) software mixed** — well-penetrated seat-based SaaS (**Salesforce, ServiceNow, Workday**) pressured as customers cut headcount/seats/app-budget growth and resist seat-plus-usage transitions, but model-neutral firms can win as independent "token optimizers" (**Palantir AIP Evolve** the template — one model swap cut token costs **97%**, 90% adoption in 3 weeks). **ORCL and Datadog flagged as having "outsized exposure to the success of the frontier labs."** UBS stays constructive on infra/semis and cross-references its **Cerebras (CBRS)** fast-inference initiation for the real-time-token niche (UBS "The Ramp in Token Optimization" / Keirstead-Arcuri, 2026-06-23). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued46392.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued46392.html)
- **The "burn down tokens to learn capability" CFO behavior (WFC, 2026-04-13).** At a software conf, ~30 CFOs described encouraging devs to "burn through tokens as quickly as they can… not because we're drunk sailors, but because we need to figure out what the technology is capable of" before setting 3-year headcount plans. **No CFO said headcount goes DOWN** — rather, token usage today comes "at the expense of headcount GROWTH for next year." Absolute token bills still small (Braze and Rubrik each reported **sub-$1M token spend**), even though "token costs have tripled since the start of the year and probably have to double from here." Anthropic "becoming more of the IBM" — safe to name in meetings (WFC "software" call, 2026-04-13). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_13_software_wfc_13_apr_26.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_13_software_wfc_13_apr_26.html)
- **Cheaper models aren't free — the scaffolding tax (Poio expert, WFC, 2026-05-29).** Matt Slotnick (ex-Google Cloud): you CAN move workloads off Claude to cheaper models (e.g. Kimi 2.6) for known workloads, "but you're going to have to build a lot more scaffolding around it… handhold it more." Frontier "is always going to be super valuable" for harder/unstructured problems; expects more companies to build their own smaller/tuned models as workload shapes become known (Baseten/Fireworks inference growth cited) — but discovery still needs frontier (WFC agentic-software call, 2026-05-29). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_29_wfc_sw_agentic_29_may_2026.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_29_wfc_sw_agentic_29_may_2026.html)
- **"Positive sum, not zero sum" — the software-bull rebuttal (Jefferies/Brian, 2026-06-01).** Pushback on the "semis win, software loses, every dollar to Anthropic comes out of Workday" framing: no past tech cycle was negative for net employment or made high-tech incumbents day-one losers; **SaaS (predictable cost) is a better customer business model than consumption** ("no bank agrees their bill will be $1M or $20M"). Incremental AI budget increasingly "comes out of human capital" (flat/down headcount funding higher AI spend) — Jefferies' own CIO said his AI budget now comes from the CEO and future budget "will come from human capital." Names ServiceNow / Intuit as SaaS that survives ("AI killed really crappy companies… ServiceNow's not crappy"). Also: the frontier-lab productization gap — getting an accountant to set up "a Mac Mini + Open Claw + Claude Code" is "in my view zero" near-term (Jefferies "Brent/Jeff AI", 2026-06-01). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_01_brent_jeff_ai_1_jun_26.html)
- **GitHub Copilot super-users + the seat→usage inflection moment (UBS MSFT recap, 2026-04-30).** Keirstead: this MSFT call "might be remembered as the moment the pace of the shift to usage-based pricing suddenly shifted" — driven by GitHub Copilot's "sharp ramp in token usage-based revenues." UBS's own CTO anecdote: rolling Claude Code + GitHub Copilot to 15,000 programmers, **30–40% became AI-coding super-users "blowing through their credit limits."** MSFT framing the usage pivot as "TAM expansive medium-term" but with near-term P&L pressure (renewal pauses as enterprises evaluate new pricing) (UBS MSFT recap, 2026-04-30). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_ubs_recap_30_par_2026.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_ubs_recap_30_par_2026.html)
- **Apple Intelligence / Gemini — agentic monetization shifting to per-app + service tiers (Citi/Counterpoint, 2026-06-15).** Post-WWDC: Apple integrating Google Gemini as Siri's foundation; Counterpoint expects Apple to monetize not via a standalone AI subscription but by **re-pricing existing services tiers** as Apple Intelligence/Siri capability is added, and sees the path toward **per-app "pro" pricing** for AI features (mirroring how ChatGPT/agents will monetize their interface to the end user). Google already gates latest Gemini behind higher Google One AI tiers (incl. a new $100/mo plan) (Citi AAPL WWDC call, 2026-06-15). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_15_aapl_wwdc_citi_15_jun_26.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_15_aapl_wwdc_citi_15_jun_26.html)
- **But enterprises have flipped from max-spend to cost-control.** Big Technology Podcast / Ramp lead economist Eric Kharazian (research_library, 2026-06-18): "We went through the token-maxing era… now businesses are saying we need to actually rein in token spend or at least understand it." Per-business AI token spend up **15x** Jan-2025→May-2026; still **+14% m/m** last month — the pullback is "on the margin" and "not happening fast enough to pull back the rising slope."
- **Spend levels remain small / early.** Ramp data (2026-06-18): top-1% of firms spend **$7,449/employee/month** on AI (software engineers ~$15-16k); top-10% **$611**; **median just $11**. Even for the top quartile, AI is only ~**2% of non-payroll spend**. 54% of US firms now pay for AI in some form. Read: huge runway, not a saturation/air-pocket story yet.
- **Multi-vendor is the new normal — the opposite of SaaS lock-in.** Top-1% AI spenders use **8 vendors** on average vs. ~2 for the median (Ramp, 2026-06-18). "Should I buy OpenAI or Anthropic?" is the wrong question — advanced users buy both and route per-task.
- **MSFT is repricing the seat model toward usage.** Axios via morning briefing (2026-06-17): Microsoft moving **Copilot Cowork to usage pricing** and **considering a hosted DeepSeek V4** as a cheaper option — "fits the tokenmaxxing pullback narrative." Earlier (briefing 2026-06-18, @zerohedge): MSFT weighing DeepSeek to replace expensive OpenAI/Anthropic APIs, raising MSFT-OpenAI partnership ROI questions. Jefferies/Brent Thill NDR (2026-06-12): MSFT framing "per user + consumptive" agentic pricing as the "best TAM ever."
- **DeepSeek/open-source is rising but overrated.** Ramp (2026-06-18): DeepSeek among fastest-growing vendors, but only **~0.4% of businesses** use it (up from 0.1%); open-source platforms at **5% of firms** (up from 1% a year ago). History rhymes — DeepSeek spiked in early 2025 then collapsed to ~0.1% once OpenAI/Anthropic cut prices; security/reputational concerns cap Western adoption.
- **The cost-control product gap = a wedge for Google.** Ramp (2026-06-18): neither OpenAI nor Anthropic has built tools that let firms manage/cap token spend (80% of their business revenue is token-based, so they're incentivized the other way). **Google is "underrated"** — diversified revenue means it can offer routing/cost-control and even run AI as a loss-leader to pull Cloud; Gemini already distributed via Workspace.
- **Model-routing is becoming standard practice.** BG2Pod (2026-06-12): enterprise survey of 300 companies — firms routing "back-of-house" tasks (KYC, customer service) to cheaper/open-source models while keeping high-value work (coding) on frontier; Harvey used a router + RL on an open-source model to beat Opus 4.7/4.8 "at a lower cost." Allen Roush (equity_calls, 2026-04-28): frontier labs are **profitable on per-token API pricing** and lose money on the $20/$200 subscriptions — "I don't think they're overpricing."
- **The "tokenmaxxing" label also has an employee-behavior edge.** Amazon set weekly AI-usage targets for >80% of devs, tracked on internal leaderboards, with employees using "MeshClaw" to automate pointless tasks to pad stats (FT via briefings, AMZN.md 2026-05-12); @zerohedge tied it to Anthropic ARR padding and Claude's alleged ~$500M "mystery bill" (2026-05-29). META cracking down on employee token usage on surging cost, pivoting to in-house models (Vitalknowledge via META.md).
- **AI is now a *meaningful* revenue contributor at the hyperscalers — the monetization is showing up.** MSFT **AI ARR reached $37B** last quarter (Azure AI + SaaS Copilot, +123% Y/Y, >11% of total revenue growing triple-digits) — "AI has become a meaningful contributor to top-line growth and not a headwind"; M365 Copilot at 20M paid seats and accelerating. Google revenue from products built on GenAI models grew **~800% Y/Y**, first-party models now process **>16B tokens/min** (vs 10B last quarter), Gemini Enterprise paid MAUs +40% Q/Q. The open monetization question Bernstein poses: **"When will AI drive increased cloud IT budgets?"** (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **The monetization narrative has flipped from "can adoption justify capex?" to "is there enough compute to serve demand?"** JPM's "Monetization From Belief To Cost" thesis: six months ago the existential worry was demand; now corporates are **blowing through entire 2026 AI budgets in 1Q26** (Microsoft and Uber reportedly exhausted full-year 2026 token budgets within months). Claude Cowork/Claude Code "changed the game on monetization," ChatGPT to the upside. The new fear is that **adoption stalls because cost-to-end-user rises even as per-token cost falls** — agentic workloads triggered a *logarithmic* jump in token usage while compute adds only a *linear* token-cost reduction; "we probably need a year or two for linear reductions to catch up to the agentic step change" (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **Hard token-volume marks confirm the Jevons dynamic.** Google now processes **~3.2 quadrillion tokens/month** across surfaces (~7x Y/Y, disclosed at I/O 2026); OpenAI API token processing went from ~300M/min to **>6B/min (~20x)** through late 2025, ChatGPT WAU ~100M (Oct-2023) → **>900M (Mar-2026, ~9x)**, run-rate revenue **>$25B (vs ~$20B Nov-2025)**; Anthropic run-rate **fast-approaching $50B (from ~$9B at YE2025)**. Per-token inference costs are falling **60–70%/yr** but usage more than re-absorbs it (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **"AI's S-curve is steepening" — model improvements are driving inference GM *expansion*, not compression (Barclays, 2026-06-01).** A core tokenmaxxing datapoint: Barclays now models the three leading labs at **>$130B revenue in CY26 and ~$200B exit-ARR** (a milestone they previously expected in early 2028; Anthropic $9B→$47B YTD +422%, OpenAI $20B→$33B +65%, "Other" $15B→$40B). The unit-economics turn is the new thesis leg — **recent model improvements deliver both better token efficiency per task *and* higher revenue per token, driving inference gross-margin expansion for Anthropic and OpenAI** (Barclays models inference margin jumping from ~34% in 2025 to ~65% from 2026 on). Compounding it at the infra layer: the **shift from "bare metal" to "inference-as-a-service" at the hyperscalers — especially those with custom silicon — is lifting margins** (cited AWS's 37.7% 1Q operating margin). Barclays explicitly bats down the cost-control bear: token-cost margin worries are "premature," large-cap tech headcount is flat-to-down while AI spend surges (SG&A leverage shows up in tech first), and Cursor's data showing p90 developers consuming **12x more tokens than the median** is "an accurate portrayal of productivity," not token-maxxing. Anthropic itself says token-maxxing "isn't a material factor driving growth" (Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening", 2026-06-01). [Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening" (2026-06-01)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html)
- **Token budgets / agent workflows are now the *entry point* for enterprise productivity budgets (FundaAI, 2026-06-24).** FundaAI frames the model layer as an Anthropic-OpenAI duopoly "jointly validating willingness-to-pay for agentic workflows, rapidly migrating enterprise software, professional-services, and knowledge-work budgets onto **token budgets**." Anthropic validated high-ARPU coding agents (avg monthly Claude spend **$211/user, 8.4x OpenAI's $25**; ~80% of revenue from enterprise contracts; NDR >500%; 9 of Fortune 10 are customers); OpenAI is validating that agents enter the broader white-collar market via distribution + role plugins (Codex expanded into IBD/equity-research/sales/data-analysis — "62 apps + 110 skills"). The monetization mechanism for the inference supply side: **as per-token cost falls, workflows that previously lacked ROI (full-repo code reading, long-context retrieval, multi-round debugging, multi-agent coordination) enter production and total token usage scales 2–3x — a Jevons effect where usage growth outweighs the unit-cost drop, so GPU/HBM/network/power demand net-grows** even as open-source (GLM-5.2 at ~1/5 Opus list price) compresses the model-layer *premium*. Critically, FundaAI estimates the true COGS gap is small (Opus API GM ~75–85% vs GLM ~10–20%; on a COGS basis Opus ~$3.8–6.3/Mtok vs GLM ~$3.5–4.0/Mtok) — so list-price gaps mostly compress Opus's API premium, not the underlying compute bill (FundaAI "Deep|LLM 26H1 Update Part 2", 2026-06-24). [FundaAI "Deep|LLM 26H1 Update Part 2: Frontier Labs, Chinese Model Vendors & the Compute Bottleneck" (2026-06-24)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LLM_Part_2_funda.html)
- **Framework — why models need reasoning + the token economics of inferencing (Bernstein, 2026-06-22).** Bernstein's "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook" lays out the agentic-AI software architecture as a framework (not fresh numbers): the token economics of inferencing, why frontier models need reasoning to unlock high-value tasks, and how agentic software is architected — the conceptual scaffolding behind the tokenmaxxing demand curve the dated bullets above quantify (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook", 2026-06-22). [Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook" (2026-06-22)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html)
- **Operator view: tokens are now "the new hot commodity" — infrastructure layer transitioning from streaming pixels to streaming intelligence (Vercel CEO, 2026-06-29).** Guillermo Rauch (Vercel founder/CEO) at Stanford MS&E435: "tokens are the new hot commodity... instead of streaming pixels, we're streaming intelligence in the form of tokens." Vercel launched "AI Gateway" — framed as a **CDN for tokens**: frontier-model tokens (Claude/Gemini/GPT) need the same CDN primitives as web pages — observability, failover, caching, security, and load-balancing (routing a cheap model for trivial tasks like "say you're welcome" rather than burning frontier GPUs). Seat-based → token-based pricing transition confirmed at the platform layer: "you've probably heard about the death of SaaS and the seat-based pricing model, and the rise of the token-based pricing model, which measures intelligence." Agentic deployment volume inflection cited: "since October last year, especially with the arrival of Opus 4.5, we started to see more and more people deploying to our platform" — Vercel became "the peanut butter and jelly of coding agents." Infrastructure compute is evolving from short HTTP request/response (ms latency) to long-running agentic tasks (seconds → minutes → hours → a full day for some platforms), requiring an entirely new "agentic infrastructure" stack. (Guillermo Rauch, Stanford MS&E435 "Economics of the AI Supercycle", 2026-06-29. Transcript: [401_Stanford_MSE435_Economics_of_-_vercel_ceo.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/401_Stanford_MSE435_Economics_of_-_vercel_ceo.html))
- **Vercel operator view, expanded — demand marks, "agent engine optimization," and the SaaS-layer split (Vercel CEO, 2026-06-29; same Stanford MS&E435 source).** (a) **Demand inflection, quantified:** Vercel compute **~3x in a few months**, **daily deployments ~2x since January**, and agent-driven usage is breaking old rate limits — Rauch's provocation is **"no more rate limits"** (consumption-based; you shouldn't cap paying customers, abuse/KYC aside). Coding agents "love to deploy" — unlike humans they don't leave code sitting local — making Vercel highly levered to **Claude Code / Codex / v0 / Grok CLI** volume. (b) **Software-creation TAM 10–100x:** agents let non-engineers ship working apps, so "**writing code is no longer the scarce thing; deploying and running useful software is**." (c) **Agentic infra = three legs:** infra *for* coding agents (deploy/sandbox/domains/APIs); infra *to build* agents (Vercel's own support agent handles **93% of inquiries**); infra *automated by* agents (cloud as a "self-driving car"). If AWS were invented today it'd be "**Amazon Agent Services**." (d) **"Agent engine optimization" — a new distribution game:** agents prefer composable, well-documented, training-data-popular tools with strong **"local reasoning"** (modifiable without the whole codebase in context) — in one tested setup **shadcn ~90%** of UI-component picks and **Vercel ~100%** of deployment picks (Next.js/React/Tailwind stack). AEO = the new SEO. (e) **SaaS split:** systems of record likely survive; **generic UI/application layers are vulnerable** (a CEO vibe-coded a parking-lot tool to drop a vendor — cf. the CRM read-through). Survivors expose **APIs/MCP/CLI/headless + consumption pricing + instant sign-up**; losers run closed workflows, sales-heavy procurement, "e-brochure" sites. (f) **Agent cybersecurity = a brand-new category** (sandbox isolation, data-exfil prevention, governance/guardrails) — the PC-era "viruses & phishing" analogue; read-through to PANW/CRWD. **Long:** companies moving "at the speed of tokens" (instant usage, consumption pricing, open interfaces). **Short:** static content/data businesses (Stack-Overflow-style Q&A), no-code/drag-and-drop builders that treat code as scary, closed enterprise SW with slow sales cycles, and any SaaS lacking API/MCP/CLI/agent access. (Guillermo Rauch, Stanford MS&E435, 2026-06-29; same transcript as above.)
- **Chris Wood (Jefferies) plays tokenmaxxing via DRAM: "falling token prices should lead to rising DRAM prices" (JEF, 2026-06-25).** In his GREED & fear newsletter, Christopher Wood offers the clearest portfolio-level bet on the tokenmaxxing Jevons thesis: he sells Alphabet (AI model layer) and buys **SK Hynix (new 4% position in three portfolios) + Samsung (+1ppt)**, explicitly arguing that "the good news about the potential for plunging token costs, signalled by the growing share taken by China players, is that, based on the Jevons Paradox, it should trigger ever greater demand for compute. Or in other words **falling token prices should lead to rising DRAM prices**." He frames Korean memory as a structural re-rating story: "The story that the DRAM industry has changed structurally, and that the companies should now be valued on a price to earnings basis rather than a price to book basis, looks to GREED & fear an increasingly powerful argument. And by this measure these companies still look cheap." Consumer/enterprise bifurcation: LLMs are "commoditised" at the consumer level; but for corporates, cheaper tokens incentivize moving small language models on-prem to protect data — driving compute/hardware demand in both cases. Bear thesis on the AI trade: malinvestment is the most likely "end of the AI trade" trigger (not a demand collapse), but for now "there remains zero sign of AI capex slowing." (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Chris_Wood.html)
- **"Less than 1% penetrated" — Morgan Stanley stays constructive on Jevons Paradox despite tokenmaxxing pullback (MS, 2026-06-26).** Stephen Byrd (MS Head of Thematic Research) and Tom Wig (TMT Specialty Sales) frame the tokenmaxxing episode as "a natural, healthy phase of early adoption" with corporate AI still "less than 1% penetrated." Byrd remains constructive on the compute cycle: falling unit token costs (~75% drop estimated from Nvidia Blackwell to Feynman) drive an explosion in demand, consistent with Jevons Paradox. Concrete enterprise ROI: enterprises save **~$55 for $2-3 in tokens** (based on $5/M tokens); a 6,000-line codebase coded for **$58 ($1/100 lines)**, saving thousands of dollars vs. manual work — the quality-vs-cost ROI that sustains frontier-model demand even under cost pressure. Key MS quality thesis: "A token is not a token is not a token" — mission-critical enterprise (coding, legal) stays on frontier American models where higher accuracy justifies the premium; routine tasks may shift to cheaper models. New risk flagged: orchestration layers ("Open Router Fusion"-type tools) that "cobble together" multiple cheaper models to match frontier performance could commoditize frontier models and transfer value to hyperscalers — "the jury is out." Geopolitical bifurcation emerging: US/West enterprise → frontier Western models; Global South / parts of Europe → open-source/Chinese alternatives ("Apple vs Android"). Investment read: bull for semiconductors/compute (Jevons demand), frontier labs for enterprise segment, hyperscalers building orchestration; mixed for pure-play frontier labs if orchestration commoditizes; global market split is the medium-term to watch (Morgan Stanley / Stephen Byrd "Discussion on tokenmaxxing", 2026-06-26). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_26_stephen_byrd_on_tokenmaxxing.html)
- **2027 = the "improving tokenomics" inflection as new compute/memory architectures ramp (BofA, 2026-05-13).** BofA's "AI 2030" frames 2026 as a year of accelerating AI sales/ROI (OpenAI + Anthropic IPOs as catalysts) and **2027 as the year of improving tokenomics/efficiency as new-architecture compute/memory systems ramp** — directionally consistent with Barclays' inference-GM-expansion thesis (BofA "AI 2030", 2026-05-13). [BofA "AI 2030" (2026-05-13)](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_State_of_the_union.html)
- **Inference cost is collapsing fast — the supply side of "tokenmaxxing."** SemiAnalysis InferenceX on DeepSeek V4: serving on **GB300 NVL72 with MTP hits $0.156 per 1M output tokens** at 50 tok/s/user (8k in / 1k out) — the rack-scale NVL72 scale-up domain "mogs" B200/B300 (8-GPU NVLink islands over IB) and MI355X. On a B200, token throughput per all-in provisioned MW rose **~300k → ~500k tok/s/MW (~1.7x) in ~6 weeks** purely from software (MegaMoE FP4 GEMMs, wider EP) at a fixed ~2.17 kW/GPU envelope — tokens-per-MW is "the best figure of merit for ROI at fleet scale" (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0→43", 2026-06-09).
- **Open-source models now reach near-parity at Day 0 — commoditization is real but China-gated.** DeepSeek V4 is the first major open model with **first-class Day-0 support on both NVIDIA CUDA *and* Huawei Ascend (950DT "David")** — a sharp change from DeepSeek v3/R1 (CUDA-only Day 0). AMD's ROCm/ATOM was disappointing at launch but improved **>100x in <1 month** (MI355X exceeding H200 at low interactivity); Kimi K2.6 still beats NVDA's Nemotron 3 Ultra on coding. The model itself co-designed for cheaper inference: CSA+HCA attention drives **50x KV-cache reduction at 1M context** (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0→43", 2026-06-09). Read-through: cheaper, more-portable open weights deepen the routing/substitution wedge — but Ascend Day-0 support is a China-domestic story.
- **Coding assistants are the monetization engine — and post-training (RL) is the cost.** SemiAnalysis: coding assistants are "the greatest B2B SaaS app the world has ever seen" — **$30B+ ARR across the six largest players today, on track to clear $100B by year end** (Tokenomics model). The agentic capability that monetizes (Claude Opus 4.8: 69.2% SWE-bench Pro, 74.6% Terminal-Bench 2.1) comes from RL, which Dario Amodei says scales log-linearly with training time — "enormously expensive," so RL-system efficiency caps how far capability can go (SemiAnalysis "RL Systems: Mind the Gap", 2026-06-16).
- **RL-training economics: managed platforms are ~2–5x cheaper than DIY, which bounds who can afford frontier post-training.** SemiAnalysis H200 TCO is **$1.59/hr/GPU** (72.5% capital, $45.2k upfront/logical GPU, 10.25% WACC / 6-yr life). On Qwen3-235B RL runs, DIY open-source stacks land **$16.23/Mtok (slime, ~4.86x) and $6.90/Mtok (Prime RL, ~2.01x)** vs Thinking Machines **Tinker's published $3.43–4.86/Mtok** — Tinker's edge attributed mainly to **LoRA multi-tenancy** (batching training across users + backfilling straggler slots) plus likely Blackwell + PD-disaggregation. RL systems are generation-bound: trainers idle 30–74% of wall-clock waiting on the generator, so **inference efficiency is the dominant lever in training cost too** (SemiAnalysis "RL Systems: Mind the Gap", 2026-06-16).
- **Long-running agentic models are the new monetization frontier — benchmarks may be the wrong axis.** BG2Pod (2026-06): Anthropic Fable 5 / Mythos and GPT-5.5 are "a new class" defined by long-running tasks; Karpathy/Noam Brown argue snapshot benchmarks are losing relevance — "the X axis has to be time or tokens or compute" because frontier models can now solve most problems if left to run. Concrete monetization proof: Stripe's **50M-line Ruby codebase refactored in a day** (Anthropic example). Implication: more compute → longer agent runs → more tokens "as far as the eye can see" — structurally bullish token demand (BG2 podcast, 2026-06).
- **xAI/SpaceX has emerged as a top-tier compute monetizer — a new tokenmaxxing supply node.** BG2Pod (2026-06): SpaceX went from non-hyperscaler to **#4 hyperscaler in ~30 days** via Anthropic + Google compute deals, monetizing at **~$22–23B/GW/yr (Anthropic) and ~$50B/GW/yr (Google) vs ~$14B/GW/yr implied** by leaked SpaceX AI revenue — i.e. selling compute at premium rates; Cursor acquisition gives xAI a frontier-model path (Composer 2.5 Pareto-dominant on Cursor-bench) (BG2 podcast, 2026-06).
- **Margins are inflecting — the returns side of the ROI debate.** Google Cloud margin **inflected to ~33% in 1Q26** (from ~30% in 4Q25 and 17.8% in 1Q25; op income tripled Y/Y to $6.6B), though higher D&A from datacenter capex is a watch-item. AWS op margins **resilient in the low-to-mid 30s** (~38% in Q1, +270bps Q/Q) even as infra/depreciation costs rise, with custom silicon a key efficiency offset. The counterweight: Bernstein models **$623B cash capex across the 4 US hyperscalers in 2026** (~$760B incl. Meta) vs **~$635B of CFO** — implying most hyperscalers need external (debt/equity) funding for the build-out, sharpening the returns-on-capex debate (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).

- **The consumer / on-device leg of the Jevons trade (audit add, Jun 2026).** The datacenter framing has a mirror in the device chain: on-device AI is driving a DRAM content step-up — **9GB on the base A20 iPhone / iOS 27** (not just Pro; Ming-Chi Kuo, 2026-06-27) — while Apple agreed a **~+20% mobile DRAM price hike for Q3** and lobbied US Commerce for access to blacklisted **CXMT** amid an acute LPDDR5 shortage (StoneX/TrendForce memory call, 2026-06-23). The pass-through is now consumer-visible: Apple raised **MacBook/iPad prices 15–25%** citing "extraordinary component shortage" from datacenter memory demand — a **~$263B single-day market-cap loss** (briefing 2026-06-26). Micron called out **>$1B HBM4 sales already**, new fabs across the US/Taiwan/Singapore/Japan and large EUV orders; BofA raising memory forecasts. Read-through: the same "falling token prices → rising DRAM prices" mechanism Chris Wood plays at the datacenter level (see above) is now showing up in the consumer hardware chain — memory, not compute, as the binding constraint.
- **Cost-control is now concrete CTO-level policy, not just survey sentiment (audit add).** Beyond the macro backlash: Anthropic enterprises are cutting Claude bills **90%+** via Sonnet-default / Opus-only-for-hardest routing plus prompt caching (briefing 2026-06-27); **Block/Square** mandates cheap models by default — engineers must *opt into* frontier ("don't take a Ferrari to the grocery store," BofA conference wrap 2026-06-08); **Uber** burned its full 2026 AI budget in 4 months then capped spend at **$1,500/person/month per tool**, and **Priceline** renewed Cursor at **+4–5×** (FundaAI Deep|LLM Pt 1, 2026-06-23). A new vector: AI **bill-audit** — startup **Vaudit** found **~$1.7M of improper charges across $34M of audited Claude spend (~5%)**, framing enterprise AI billing opacity as its own cost-control friction (The Information, 2026-06-25).
- **The China cost gap, quantified — and DeepSeek's war chest (audit add).** The substitution wedge is sharper on price than the dossier's earlier "overrated" framing implies: **DeepSeek V4 Flash $0.1/Mtok vs GPT-5.5 $4.4 vs Fable 5 $7.7** (Chinese APIs <20% of US pricing); **4 of 5 top OpenRouter models are now Chinese**; **Z.ai's GLM-5.2 compressed the quality gap to near-parity in ~2 weeks** (briefings 2026-06-20/06-22/06-26). **DeepSeek raised $7.4B (>$50B valuation)** — its biggest-ever first raise, reportedly triggered by Anthropic's Mythos preview (briefing 2026-06-26). The mitigant still holds (adoption gated by security/reputation, ~0.4% of firms), and FundaAI's COGS work caps the threat: the *true* compute-cost gap is small (Opus ~$3.8–6.3 vs GLM ~$3.5–4.0/Mtok), so list-price gaps compress Opus's **premium**, not the underlying compute bill.
- **Supply-side econ: efficiency accrues to the hyperscaler, not the infra provider (CoreWeave/Cantor expert, 2026-06-18).** GB300 spot ~$7–8/GPU-hr ≈ **~$0.14/Mtok**; GPU-hour pricing holds steady across generations but per-token cost falls with throughput gains — so the **hyperscaler/customer captures the efficiency**, not the neocloud (CoreWeave targets ~25% EBITDA). Long-term capacity contracts inflecting **$10–12M → $15–16M/MW**; **GPU useful life stretched from 3–4yr to 7–8yr** (a capex-payback tailwind); Anthropic the anchor customer behind premium $/GW-hr deals. [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_call_coreweave_cantor.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_call_coreweave_cantor.html)
- **GPU-rental economics on Vera Rubin — the cost-floor-vs-value-ceiling framework (SemiAnalysis, @SemiAnalysis_ thread 4/4, ~2026-07-10).** SemiAnalysis frames GPU rental as a band between a **cost-based floor** (what it costs to provision the GPU) and a **value-based ceiling** (what the workload is worth to the renter). On **VR NVL72 (Vera Rubin)** those are roughly **$4.92/hr/GPU (floor)** vs **$12.25/hr/GPU (ceiling)** — a wide **~2.5×** band, and "where the market settles is the actual question for the next two years." Contextualizes the other $/GPU-hr marks on this page: the **GB300 spot ~$7–8/GPU-hr** (Cantor, above) and the **H100 spot ~$2.42/hr** (bear thread below) sit inside analogous bands. The width of the floor↔ceiling gap is the pricing-power / margin swing factor for the neoclouds ([CRWV](../CRWV.md)/[NBIS](../NBIS.md)) and a demand-durability tell — a narrowing toward the floor would signal softening compute demand; holding near the ceiling signals scarcity. _(Tweet-thread fragment, 4/4; references a fuller SemiAnalysis article — date approximate.)_
- **Inference moving off merchant GPUs — alt-silicon & lab vertical integration (audit add).** **OpenAI launched "Jalapeño,"** its first in-house LLM-optimized chip (TSMC 3nm; Jalapeño2 on A16 + CoWoS); **Sol 5.6 ran on Cerebras at 750 tok/s**; **SambaNova is raising $1B at $10B (5× in 4 months)** (company-specific briefings 2026-06-27/28). Each reshapes *who captures token economics* as inference leaves the merchant-GPU stack. Framework backing: FundaAI's **Part 1** (companion to the already-cited Part 2) lays out the **Agent Scaling Law** and the ROI-discipline inflection — Fable 5 priced **$10/$50 per Mtok (~2× Opus 4.8)** with an output-token premium, the Silicon Data LLM Token Expenditure Index pullback read as a **"premium pullback, not demand collapse,"** the Linux Foundation **"Tokenomics Foundation"** cross-vendor metering standard, and Salesforce's shift to **"Agentic Work Units"** (FundaAI Deep|LLM 26H1 Pt 1, 2026-06-23). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/FundaAI_LLM.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/FundaAI_LLM.html)
- **"Was widespread tokenmaxxing ever really here?" — the on-the-ground rebuttal (SemiAnalysis "TokenBudgeting", 2026-06-30).** SemiAnalysis (Huang/Brookhart/Patel) talked to **>50 enterprises** (Slack/phone/Databricks AI Summit) and concludes the viral Meta/Uber tokenmaxxing stories are *overstated* — products of poor incentives and lax oversight, "not an absence of high-ROI activities." The Meta "Claudeconomics" dashboard (top-250 power users; **>60T tokens over 30 days, single user ~280B**, gamed via "Token Legend"/"Cache Wizard") was shut down 2 days after The Information reported it. Their **Tokenomics Model** read: **>70% of OpenAI+Anthropic ARR is coding** (Anthropic 90%+ B2B vs OpenAI ~60%); the **Total Coding Market ARR is $30B+ today heading to ~$80B**, and **TaaS/API endpoint providers (Together/Fireworks/Baseten) are >$4B ARR** with AWS Bedrock estimates driving total AWS growth "well above street." **Budgets are the new norm but with no consensus number — from $250/mo (top-3 aero/defense; top-3 pharma $500) to ~$2,000/mo (Workday, Stripe) up to tens of thousands** (data scientists get the largest allocations); one $300B-AUM asset manager took Claude usage from 50%→90-100% of workdays in 6 months, Sonnet-default with Opus for coding. Employees "game" M365 Copilot's free unlimited chat to draft/synthesize before spending metered Claude/Codex tokens. The punchline datapoint: **Anthropic's own docs put average Claude Code usage at $150-250/dev/month, only 10% of users >$30/day** — "there is not a material risk present to 2H26 AI budgets… we expect Anthropic and OpenAI's API business to continue to grow at their current net-new rates m/m." [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_on_tokenmaxxing.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_on_tokenmaxxing.html)
- **The token economy, deduplicated — the definitive size marks (Exponential View / Azeem Azhar, 2026-06-25).** Azhar's team ("The State of the AI Economy," bottom-up across 1,000+ firms, revenue counted once via value-add so the same dollar isn't double-counted) puts **deduplicated GenAI ecosystem revenue at ~$175bn annualized** (from $110bn trailing-12mo — an 18x gap that reflects how fast the run-rate is inflecting), vs **~$2T cumulative hyperscaler+neocloud capex through 2026E** — framing the central question as "*can cheapening AI create enough volume and margin to service the buildout?*" On tokens: **global inference volume now exceeds 30 quadrillion/month (+~14x Y/Y)**, and the chat→agent transition is the multiplier — an **agentic-coding task consumes ~4.17M tokens (~1,200x a multi-turn chat task's ~3.4k)**, while agent-coordination density (tool-use per prompt on OpenRouter) rose ~5%→15% in 1H26. The Jevons mechanism, quantified: **blended price/Mtok fell $17→$2** even as the Epoch capability index rose **112→158** and tokens spent "thinking" per output token went **12→36** — cheaper + better models "make previously uneconomical applications viable." (Exponential View, 2026-06-25; note: this is Azeem Azhar's Exponential View, not a16z.) [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ev-state-of-ai-economy-2026.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ev-state-of-ai-economy-2026.html)
- **Per-GW rental economics + the "Opus margin >80%" fact that underwrites premium-rate compute buys (Dylan Patel @ Sequoia, 2026-06).** In a long Sequoia interview, Dylan Patel gives the clearest public per-GW rental marks: **Trainium rents to Anthropic/OpenAI at sub-$10B/GW/yr** (floor-mechanism deal, cancellable if underperforming), **GPUs historically ~$12-13B/GW** (Amazon subsidized lower), and the **SpaceX/Google deal at ~$25B/GW/yr ($25M/MW; Google paying ~$11/GPU-hr to xAI)** — "a crazy divergence." Why frontier labs can pay above-market for every GPU: **Anthropic's per-token margin on an Opus 4.8 API token is "north of 80%"** (total corporate GM clawed down a bit by Bedrock/Vertex deals), Anthropic is **net-income positive ex-SBC in Q2 and possibly incl-SBC by Q3** — so "double the cost of compute, I'm still at 50% GM and my NOI still goes up." Cost curve: **model cost for equivalent quality falling ~60x/yr**, ~40x/yr on intelligence-per-watt; Hopper→Blackwell ~30x on DeepSeek. Demand read: Fable 5/Mythos 5 is "not just 2x Opus's TAM" — the TAM of useful tasks expanded far faster than world compute did over the last 6-8 months, "which is why prices go up." Bull-case governor stated plainly: this holds "if the work these models can do expands faster than compute capacity" (the "dark GDP" thesis); ~20 GW of datacenter deploying in 2026, >30 GW in 2027; OpenAI+Anthropic combined **>100 GW by 2030**. [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Dylan_sequoia.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Dylan_sequoia.html)
- **GLM-5.2 = the first open-weight model to reach the frontier's doorstep on agentic coding — the substitution wedge, sharpened (TheValueist, 2026-06-21; "Uncovering GLM 5.2" / Devansh, 2026-06-26).** Z.ai's GLM-5.2 (1M-context, **MIT-licensed**, ~744B-total/40B-active MoE) **trails Claude Opus 4.8 by only 0.7pt on FrontierSWE (74.4 vs 75.1) and beats GPT-5.5 (72.6)**; external FrontierSWE leaderboard puts it at **74% dominance — behind Opus 4.8 (75%) but ahead of GPT-5.5 (73%), Gemini 3.1 Pro (40%), and DeepSeek V4 Pro (29%)**; 81.0 Terminal-Bench 2.1, 62.1 SWE-bench Pro, 1524 Elo on GDPval-AA (level with GPT-5.5). Headline pricing is the wedge: **$1.40/$4.40 per Mtok in/out (Z.ai) vs Opus 4.8 $5.00/$25.00 — ~3.6x cheaper input, ~5.7x cheaper output**, i.e. "**roughly one-sixth the per-token cost of GPT-5.5**." The two caveats that keep the frontier's premium intact: (1) **on a cost-per-successful-task basis the gap narrows** — GLM-5.2 is token-hungry (~43k output tokens/Intelligence-Index task, ~$0.46/task vs DeepSeek V4 Pro's $0.05), and (2) Opus stays **materially ahead on the hardest long-horizon reliability** (SWE-Marathon: 26.0 vs 13.0 — where "compounding errors, tool orchestration, sustained execution" dominate). Architecturally it's an efficiency story, not just scale: DeepSeek Sparse Attention + **IndexShare (cross-layer index reuse, −2.9x attention FLOPs at 1M context)** + improved MTP (accept-length 4.56→5.47) + critic-based PPO via "slime" RL — designed to attack both sides of the inference-cost equation. Read-through: reinforces the dossier's "list-price gaps compress the frontier *premium*, not the compute bill" thesis (cf. FundaAI), accelerates model-layer commoditization below the absolute frontier and value-shift toward orchestration/workflow ownership — but adoption is gated by **Zhipu being on the US Entity List (since 2025-01-16)**, which raises US-enterprise procurement/vendor-risk friction (the geopolitical bifurcation MS's Byrd also flags). [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/21_TheValueist_on_X___GLM-52__Strategic_Read-Through_for_Models_Agents_and_Infrastructure_New__6_21_26____X.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/21_TheValueist_on_X___GLM-52__Strategic_Read-Through_for_Models_Agents_and_Infrastructure_New__6_21_26____X.html) · [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/3_Uncovering_the_Secrets_to_GLM_52s_Amazing_Performance.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/3_Uncovering_the_Secrets_to_GLM_52s_Amazing_Performance.html)
- **The hardware mechanism behind cache-cost reduction: Anthropic's real cache pricing, and Nvidia's ICMS/Bluefield-4 platform now shipping to operationalize it (Vikram Sekar/Substack, 2026-01-18 — free-preview portion only, paywall-limited, treat as directional not exhaustive; SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09).** Sekar frames the tokenomics case for why KV-cache management is now as important to inference economics as raw GPU utilization: agentic workloads generate **~100x more tokens than human-driven use**, and a **KV-cache miss costs ~10x more than a hit** (cited to Manus AI, since acquired by Meta for $2B). Anthropic's actual cache pricing illustrates the stakes (Source: Anthropic, per Sekar, 2026-01-18): for **Opus 4.5, cache hits run $0.5/Mtok vs input $5/Mtok and output $25/Mtok**; writing to cache costs **$6.25/Mtok at a 5-min TTL or $10/Mtok at a 1-hour TTL** — a >10x hit/input spread that directly rewards whoever can keep context resident cheaply; Sonnet 4.5 carries separate (higher) long-context pricing per a second table in the source, not itemized here. Nvidia's CES 2026 **ICMS (Inference Context Memory Storage)** platform is the response — using NAND SSDs to expand KV-cache storage far beyond HBM/DRAM limits; Sekar notes it isn't novel technology (**WEKA — Val Bercovici, Chief AI Officer, the piece's technical reviewer —, VAST Data, and Dell already sell external KV-cache storage systems**) but is under-covered given the tokenomics impact. SemiAnalysis's harder technical follow-up names the actual shipping hardware: **Bluefield-4**, a DPU co-packaging a full Grace CPU (64 enabled cores, 128GB LPDDR5) with a ConnectX-9 chip, deployed as the frontend NIC on every **Rubin** compute tray; four BF4s populate Nvidia's **"Context Memory Storage Platform"** for KV-cache offload to high-speed NAND (CES 2026 slide claims "5X Higher Tokens per Second, 5X Higher Power Efficiency"; partner ecosystem: AIC, Cloudian, DDN, Dell, HPE, Hitachi Vantara, IBM, Nutanix, Pure Storage, Supermicro, VAST, WEKA). SemiAnalysis frames KV-cache offload as a **"third network"** in the AI topology (alongside East-West GPU scale-out and North-South storage/internet), with Bluefield-4 blurring "the line between a NIC with CPU compute and a CPU with networking acceleration... even more" (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back: The Datacenter CPU Landscape in 2026," pp.69-70, 2026-02-09). Read-through for this dossier: this is the concrete infrastructure layer that lets falling per-token cache costs scale without a proportional HBM/DRAM bill — the supply-side complement to the demand-side Jevons story tracked above; gap flagged rather than filled — the Sekar source's paywalled sections (full competitive/market-sizing detail) are not accessible from the free preview. [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Context_Memory_Storage_Systems_Disruption_of_Agentic_AI_Tokenomics_and_Memory_Pooling_Flash_vs_DRAM.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Context_Memory_Storage_Systems_Disruption_of_Agentic_AI_Tokenomics_and_Memory_Pooling_Flash_vs_DRAM.html) · [../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html)

## Key debates
- **★ NEW AXIS (2026-08-05) — PRODUCER PROFIT vs CONSUMER SURPLUS, which is a different question from frontier-vs-open-source and is not settled by any datapoint on this page.** FUNDA, arguing the compute-bull case, still concedes the giveaway: **"with token prices falling roughly 10x a year historically, a substantial share of AI's value may settle as CONSUMER SURPLUS RATHER THAN PRODUCER PROFIT, as in previous general-purpose technology waves"** (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05). ⚠️ **Every other debate on this page asks WHERE in the stack the value lands — frontier lab, cloud, accelerator, memory, optics. This one asks whether it lands anywhere at all, or accrues to the buyer as cheaper work. It is the one bear case that is fully compatible with all the bullish VOLUME evidence here (35 quadrillion tokens/month, Grok 4.5 tripling consumption out of the gate, AT&T at ~16.4tn tokens annualised) and simultaneously with all the bullish CAPABILITY evidence (METR ~89-day doubling, Opus 4.5 at 320 minutes). Volume and capability compounding at ~10x/yr each, against price falling ~10x/yr, is precisely the configuration in which a technology transforms an economy without enriching its producers. Falsification test: watch whether the managed-inference layer (Copilot / Bedrock / Vertex paid conversion) holds price as capability rises — that is the only place in the chain where producer capture is directly observable.**
- **Bull / where the money is:** Frontier captures the value and long-running agents expand token demand structurally — BG2Pod (2026-06-12): "two things can be true… majority of economic value accrues to the frontier, majority of tokens are open source." Bullish for the frontier labs *and* — counterintuitively — for compute: "the better open source does, the better it is for compute providers" because frontier labs capturing less margin means more spend on raw compute (Baker, 2026-06-12). NVDA bull "tokenmaxx" thesis cited by MS Sales (NVDA.md 2026-06-11). The unit-economics that *underwrite* the whole buildout: Dylan Patel (Sequoia, 2026-06) puts **Anthropic's Opus 4.8 API-token margin >80% and Anthropic net-income-positive ex-SBC in Q2** — so a lab "can pay above-market for every GPU" (double compute cost, still ~50% GM, NOI still rises), which is why per-GW rental rates diverge so widely (Trainium sub-$10B/GW vs SpaceX/Google ~$25B/GW-yr).
- **Bear / what could break it:** (1) Capital-intensive price war. Vital Knowledge (NVDA.md 2026-06-11): "OpenAI considering token price war while being capital-intensive — a price war in a capital-intensive industry is historically toxic"; Oracle F27 capex/sales >100% is "why NVDA and MU trade at such low PEs." (2) Substitution + routing caps frontier ARR if open-source closes the long-running-task gap (Jensen's model-routing argument, BG2Pod 2026-06-12). The gap is now visibly narrow: **GLM-5.2 (open-weight, MIT) trails Opus 4.8 by just 0.7pt on FrontierSWE at ~1/6 GPT-5.5's per-token cost** (TheValueist / Devansh, 2026-06-21/26) — but Opus keeps a **material lead on the hardest long-horizon reliability (SWE-Marathon 26.0 vs 13.0)** and Zhipu's US Entity-List status gates enterprise adoption, so the frontier premium compresses in mid-tier work while surviving at the top. (3) AWS read-through: Bernstein flagged a "tokenmaxxing hangover" — non-AI cloud growth flattening as compute is reallocated to AI labs, a 4Q risk (AMZN.md). (4) Chanos (NVDA.md 2026-06-14): if GPU data-center economics are as good as claimed, why not raise prices 3-4x — implying either the premium is overstated or rent is being left on the table. (5) **Demand-vs-cost ambiguity (audit add):** H100 compute spot fell to **$2.42/hr, −40% from the May peak** — SemiAnalysis flags the open read: structural cost improvement, or an early sign compute demand is waning (briefing 2026-06-27).
- **Inference efficiency is the governor on both monetization and capability.** Two SemiAnalysis pieces point the same way: serving cost per token is collapsing (GB300 at $0.156/Mtok; B200 +1.7x tokens/MW from software alone), which *expands* monetizable demand (Jevons) but compresses per-token pricing power; and RL post-training — the thing that creates the agentic capability worth paying for — is itself generation-bound, so the same inference advances cap how much capability frontier labs can afford to train. Bull read: cheaper inference → more agentic adoption → more tokens. Bear read: if open-source closes the Day-0 gap (DeepSeek V4 now Day-0 on CUDA *and* Ascend) the frontier's pricing premium erodes from both sides (SemiAnalysis "DeepSeek V4 Day 0→43", 2026-06-09; "RL Systems", 2026-06-16).
- **Timeline / inflection:** Next 12 months is "the most indicative" of whether closed-source keeps its stranglehold or open-source grabs the long tail (Clark Tang, BG2Pod 2026-06-12). Watch: MSFT Copilot usage-pricing rollout + hosted-DeepSeek decision; whether OpenAI's mooted price cuts dislodge stickiness; Ramp's next data prints on DeepSeek/open-source share.
- **Returns-on-capex is the central macro debate, and the funding gap makes it urgent.** Bernstein: the "biggest debate" is *how long can the build-out continue and when do we get proof of ROI?* AI revenue growth has accelerated but is "still early to justify the ROI on all these investments" — revenue/backlog/CFO send a *mixed* message. With 2026 capex (~$623B) now running roughly level with CFO (~$635B) and capex set to outgrow CFO in 2027, the marginal dollar of AI infra increasingly comes from capital markets — i.e. monetization has to inflect or the funding math breaks (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **Model wars as the winner/loser determinant.** Bernstein flags "do the model wars determine winners and losers?" as an open question, and observes that "who is an AI winner and who is a loser is changing quite frequently" even across the hyperscalers — the laggard (Google, vertically integrated) now "appears the leader in AI cloud." Read-through to monetization: the model-economics fight (frontier vs open-source, inference vs training) maps directly onto who captures token revenue (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).

_Private (no wiki page): **Anthropic** and **OpenAI** are the direct objects of the theme. Run-rates are inflecting hard — JPM: **Anthropic fast-approaching $50B (from ~$9B at YE2025); OpenAI >$25B (from ~$20B Nov-2025)** with API tokens >6B/min (~20x) and ChatGPT >900M WAU (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16); Barclays corroborates (**Anthropic ~$47B, OpenAI ~$33B run-rate**, three leading labs >$130B CY26 revenue / ~$200B exit-ARR — a year ahead of prior forecasts; Barclays 2026-06-01). FundaAI's read: the contest has shifted from pure model capability into a duopoly competing on **agent workflows, token budgets, and enterprise-productivity entry points**, with Anthropic cash-flow-positive at ~15–20% FCF margin / ~60–70% GM and the 2026-05-28 Series H at $965B post-money (FundaAI 2026-06-24). Both are profitable on per-token API but lose money on flat subscriptions (Allen Roush, 2026-04-28); ~80% of their business revenue is token-based, leaving them structurally unincentivized to build cost-control tooling (Ramp, 2026-06-18). **The monetization is now printing (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-08): Anthropic + OpenAI ≈ $100B combined ARR; Anthropic >$60B ARR (3Q26) with GAAP EBIT ~$1B (API 75-85% of rev, subs 15% of ARR, Consumer only ~5%; NNARR $10B+/mo; NRR ~500%; inference GM mid-60s%, Opus 4.8 API-token GM 80%+; ARR/MW ~$60M vs ~$16M 9mo ago), vs OpenAI at ~−100% EBIT dragged by ~900M free users (~$0.70/mo to serve) though its API/B2B is now the majority of monthly NNARR post-March-5.5/Codex — converging on Anthropic's consumption model.** **Thinking Machines (Tinker)** is the emerging RL-training cost leader (~$3.43–4.86/Mtok via LoRA multi-tenancy, ~2–5x cheaper than DIY open-source stacks) — a private read on where post-training economics settle (SemiAnalysis "RL Systems", 2026-06-16). **SpaceX/xAI** vaulted to #4 hyperscaler in ~30 days, monetizing compute at premium $/GW (BG2 podcast, 2026-06). Anthropic overtook OpenAI in US business adoption (~41% vs ~39.5% of firms) and is the stickiness leader via Claude Code despite frequent compute-constrained outages; the Fable 5 export-control "ban" may *accelerate* adoption via a forbidden-fruit effect (Big Technology / Ramp, 2026-06-18)._

## Management commentary — cross-company evolution (recent quarters)

| Company | AI monetization (ARR / rev) | Token / consumption volume | Pricing shift (seat → usage) | Cost control / substitution | Capex & funding strain | Cloud / AI margin |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [MSFT](../MSFT.md) | AI ARR ~$0→$37B, +123% Y/Y ↑ | GitHub commits ~1B→14B/run-rate ↑ | seats→E7/Copilot usage pricing (Jun-26) ↑ | weighing hosted DeepSeek V4 + MAI ↑ | capex $22B→$32B/q, CY26 ~$190B ↑ | MS Cloud GM 70%→66% (Q4 ~64%) ↓ |
| [GOOG](../GOOG.md) | GenAI-product rev +~800% Y/Y ↑ | ~980T→3.2 quadrillion tokens/mo ↑ | "not rushing" ads-in-AI-Mode → | underrated cost-control/routing winner ↑ | FY26 capex →$180-190B; FCF→negative ↑ | Cloud margin 17.8%→32.9% ↑ |
| [AMZN](../AMZN.md) | AI run-rate $0→>$15B; Bedrock +170% q/q ↑ | "barbelled" agentic demand exploding ↑ | — (IaaS consumption-native) → | Trainium ~30-40% cheaper price/perf ↑ | capex →$200B FY26; TTM FCF $26B→~$1B ↓ | AWS OI margin → ~38%, +270bps q/q ↑ |
| [META](../META.md) | ad tools $20B→$60B+ run-rate ↑ | adaptive ranking, trillion-param inference ↑ | — (ads-funded, internal) → | crackdown on employee tokens; MTIA/in-house ↑ | capex ~$71B→$125-145B; FCF→negative ↓ | op margin ~41%, stable → |
| [ORCL](../ORCL.md) | OCI rev +93%; RPO $99B→$638B (+363%) ↑ | GPU utilization 97.5%; >1.2GW delivered ↑ | BYOH/prepaid book →$75B ↑ | component costs passed through via contracts → | net capex $48B→~$70B; FCF deeply negative ↓ | GM down ~5pts FY26; ROIC ~20% "high 20s" ↓ |
| [CRM](../CRM.md) | Agentforce ARR $0→>$1B; AI&Data $3.4B ↑ | 3.2T→28.6T tokens (+152% q/q) ↑ | seats→A1E/A4x premium + work-units ↑ | ~$300M Anthropic spend = rising COGS ↑ | asset-light; $25bn ASR, FCF >$16.5B ↑ | non-GAAP op margin ~34.8%, +250bps ↑ |
| [NOW](../NOW.md) | Now Assist ACV $500M→$600M; AI commit $1B→$1.5B ↑ | hybrid assist-packs / WDF tokens ↑ | 50% of net-new now non-seat ↑ | inference "very low % of cost" → | asset-light; $5bn buyback + $2bn ASR ↑ | op margin ~31%→32%; GM headwind from LLM cost → |

**Read:** Monetization is showing up everywhere (every member's AI ARR/rev arrow points up) and token/consumption volume is inflecting hard — the Jevons "tokenmaxxing" demand signal is unambiguous; the divergence is on returns and pricing, where the hyperscalers building the compute (AMZN/ORCL/META/GOOG) are eating negative FCF and margin/ROIC pressure while the app layer (CRM/NOW) converts the same agentic wave into high-margin, asset-light ARR via the seat→usage repricing. The contested edge is cost-control/substitution: MSFT and META are actively building cheaper in-house/open-source paths, validating the cost-discipline backlash even as frontier spend climbs.

_Source: member-company earnings calls; see each company page for quarter-by-quarter detail._

Does it shape capacity expansion or the innovation around data center efficiency?" **The answer (Lumentum mgmt): "Lumentum is at the 'plumbing' layer of the data center, two or three steps removed from any token economics. The number they optimise is power efficiency per bit, and the route to it is higher power lasers. The more bits moved per square area of indium phosphide, the more value delivered. Speed and efficiency are the same problem to them; faster transmission is how joules per bit comes down"** — plus, on capacity, **"they are adding as fast as they can… two fabs in Japan produce most of the world's supply of EML lasers, and capacity there is up eight-fold in two and a half years… Lumentum now runs five fabs, including a new one starting to ramp that is not yet producing wafers."** **Aurelion's "Thoughts": "Hyperscalers are working on cost per token and that pressure runs all the way down to the 'plumbing'. The useful part is incentive alignment. Higher power and higher speed make the link more efficient and carry higher ASP at the same time. Lumentum gets paid to build what its customers actually need"** — plus the flag that **"eight-fold EML capacity in two and a half years cuts against a pure scarcity thesis."** **Value-migration chain: "the cost per unit of model intelligence is persistently decreasing, especially with new open source releases like Kimi K3 and Grok 4.5. If demand for tokens is elastic, which we posit it is, then consumption rises faster than price falls… models would become increasingly commoditized, with high competitive pressure and low switching costs," so "value migrates toward layers that depend on physical scarcity"** — ranked as **leading-edge foundry most scarce (TSMC effectively the sole frontier supplier) > HBM constrained across 3 suppliers but "behaves as a commodity, with more capacity expected to come online within the next 18 to 24 months" > optical/photonic components "in a similar position to memory."** **The caveat: "training is the most communication intensive workload… inference is less network intensive, or at least has been historically, so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics,"** closing via longer context windows, heavier routing, **disaggregated prefill and decode**, agentic usage and geographically distributed inference driving DCI — netting to **"optics at large are insulated from model commoditization, because they benefit from bits moved rather than tokens generated across infrastructure."** **PhotonCap's live evidence, with its own bound: "The joint inference solution AMD and Cerebras announced in July is the live example. Helios handles the front half, reading the prompt, and Cerebras handles the back half, generating the answer, so work in progress crosses a network between two machines. What that link is made of has not been disclosed. So this is evidence that inference moves a lot of data, not evidence of any vendor's revenue."**

(2) Model API on OWNED infra at 65% inference / 2,750 tokens/sec/GPU / $1.75 per mn tokens → $30.4bn rev/GW, ~75% margin, ~46% ROIC (band 19-63%). (3) Model API on 3P infra at $7.75/hr rental → $40.5bn rev/GW, ~$28bn compute cost, ~31% margin, ~25% NOPAT margin (band −9% to 32%; MS labels this ROIC "illustrative" and does not apply the 75% utilisation haircut).** **Methodology that matters: the 2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU range is a DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T, FP4, 8K-in/1K-out workload at ~50 tokens/sec/user — a workload spec, not a hardware constant; bigger models (2.8T Kimi-K3) price higher but throughput lower, so "higher pricing on larger models does not necessarily translate proportionately into higher revenue/GW"; token-price benchmark Muse Spark 1.1 blended $1.58 (8K/1K I/O); training is charged by discounting revenue-generating inference capacity while still bearing all cost.** Conclusion: attractive returns are "an invitation for competition" → more entrants/open source → a permanent premium on product and compute efficiency and on **the value of compute infrastructure assets.** Bullish AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT + GOOGL/META.

  **Also added: the AgentX workload specification (real Claude Code and Codex traces, MEDIAN INPUT 140K TOKENS, theoretical median cache-hit roofline 99.2%) — the public spec behind why cache-read pricing dominates agentic cost; and the 08-06 price action (OpenAI −80% on GPT-5.6 Luna-Max the day DeepSeek shipped, DeepSeek still 60% cheaper; CFRA's $1.80 → $0.95 → $0.04 task-cost ladder, attributed and not adopted).** Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[CEREBRAS]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[SPCX]], [china-export](china-export.md). Sources: 13D Research & Strategy "What I Learned This Week" excerpt 2026-08-06 (image-only PDF, read by page rendering; StoneX-watermarked copy) · SemiAnalysis "Ultra-High Interactivity on NVIDIA GPUs? - TileRT InferenceX" published 2026-08-09.
